## French/DefInjected/ThingDef/WA.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | French | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Aquarium.label` | Aquarium | Aquarium | Aquarium |  |
| `WA_Aquarium.description` | As it became possible to produce colorless glass at the end of the Edo era, glass aquariums became widely spread among the commoners.<br>The aquarium containing the goldfish makes the viewer cool. | As it became possible to produce colorless glass at the end of the Edo era, glass aquariums became widely spread among the commoners.<br>The aquarium containing the goldfish makes the viewer cool. | Un aquarium de poissons rouges qui invite à la contemplation. Les aquariums en verre se sont répandus avec l'amélioration de la fabrication du verre à la fin de l'époque d'Edo. |  |
| `WA_Glow_01Toudai.label` | Cow dung lighthouse | Oil lamp stand | Lampe à huile sur pied |  |
| `WA_Glow_01Toudai.description` | The lighthouse lights the light with a light on the lamp core soaked in "kerosene" produced from the animal's oil.<br>It is said that it began to be used around the Heian era, and it is said that the aristocratic group used to love mainly.<br>Cow dung lighthouse has been called by this name because its base part resembles the form of cow dung. | An oil lamp with a wick resting in a shallow dish. Its traditional name refers to the shape of its base, which resembles cow dung. Such lamps were once used in aristocratic households. | Une lampe dont la mèche trempe dans de l'huile. Son socle évoque une bouse de vache, à l'origine de son nom traditionnel. Ce type d'éclairage était notamment utilisé par les aristocrates. |  |
| `WA_Glow_02Gatou.label` | Gato | Gato | Lampe gatō |  |
| `WA_Glow_02Gatou.description` | Gato is also a lighting fixture made from "kerosene" made from animal fat.<br>It began to be used around the Muromachi period, and it circulated enough to be used as a light source for the common people in the Edo period.<br>It is called a tile light because it makes it by baking it using the same soil as the Kawara.<br>It is one of the popular reasons to be flame retardant materials. | A clay oil lamp made from material similar to roof tiles. Its fire-resistant construction helped it become a common household light. | Une lampe à huile en terre cuite, fabriquée avec une argile semblable à celle des tuiles. Son matériau résiste au feu et son usage s'est répandu dans les foyers japonais. |  |
| `WA_Glow_03Andon.label` | Ariake lantern | Ariake lantern | Lanterne ariake |  |
| `WA_Glow_03Andon.description` | Lighthouses and Gato were problematic in windshields, so lanterns wrapped in Japanese paper were invented.<br>Lanterns were originally used for carrying, but since it was "kerosene" made from animal oil, there is a difficulty in portability and it will be used for indoor stationary use.<br>Ariake lanterns can be illuminated brightly when used out from the box, and when used in a box it gained popularity as an excellent one that can be used as a bedtime light with suppressed brightness. | A paper-covered lantern that shelters its flame. Outside its box it gives a brighter light; inside, it becomes a subdued night light. | Une lanterne protégée par du papier japonais. Sortie de son coffret, elle éclaire largement ; replacée à l'intérieur, elle diffuse une lumière douce pour la nuit. |  |
| `WA_Glow_04Bonbori.label` | Bonbori | Bonbori | Bonbori |  |
| `WA_Glow_04Bonbori.description` | The weak point of kerosene which is liquid is easy to spill and lack portability.<br>So lighting using candles that will not spill will be accepted.<br>Snow cave is made by covering a candle on a candlestick with paper or cloth, and something of various shapes such as square, hexagon, octagon, round shape etc are made.<br>Even now there are habits left to decorate Bonbori during events such as dolls' festival. | A lantern made by covering a frame with paper or cloth around a candle. Bonbori still decorate traditional celebrations such as the Doll Festival. | Une lanterne dont une armature de formes variées est couverte de papier ou de tissu. Les bonbori décorent encore les fêtes traditionnelles, notamment la fête des poupées. |  |
| `WA_Glow_05Chouchin.label` | Chochin | Chochin | Chōchin |  |
| `WA_Glow_05Chouchin.description` | Chochin is also lighting with a candle, which is very light because it covers the body made of bamboo chicken and Japanese paper.<br>It became a structure that can be folded up, and because of its portability it became an alternative to lighting for portability.<br>It is said that it was made in the Azuchi-Momoyama period and spread in the Edo period.<br>Furthermore, even now the contents are different and loved and it continues to be used. | A lightweight paper lantern stretched over a folding bamboo frame. Portable and compact, it has long been used in streets and at festivals. | Une lanterne légère de papier japonais tendu sur une armature de bambou pliable. Facile à transporter, elle accompagne depuis longtemps les rues et les fêtes. |  |
| `WA_Glow_06Bonfire.label` | Bonfire | Bonfire | Feu sur brasero |  |
| `WA_Glow_06Bonfire.description` | Bonfire are one of the lighting fixtures since ancient times, mainly installed outdoors.<br>Put the fire in a steel basket with fuel like pine as a fuel.<br>In addition to being used for outdoor events such as martial forces and festivals, it also fulfills the role of a fishing light on fishing boats.<br>Even now it is used at night festival events and cormorants. | An outdoor fire held in a metal basket. It lights gatherings, festivals and some fishing boats. | Un feu extérieur contenu dans une corbeille métallique. Il éclaire les rassemblements, les fêtes et certains bateaux de pêche. |  |
| `WA_Glow_07LongBrazier.label` | Brazier | Brazier | Brasero |  |
| `WA_Glow_07LongBrazier.description` | Brazier is half-filled with burning charcoal.<br>It is one of the traditional heating devices.<br>In addition to heating, you can boil tea or tobacco pipe. | A vessel partly filled with glowing charcoal. It provides warmth and can heat tea or light a pipe. | Un récipient partiellement rempli de charbon incandescent. Il sert à chauffer la pièce, à faire chauffer le thé ou à allumer une pipe. |  |
| `WA_Seat_Zabuton_Red.label` | Zabuton(Red) | Zabuton(Red) | Zabuton rouge |  |
| `WA_Seat_Zabuton_Red.description` | The cushion contains cotton etc. as a cushioning material in a cloth bag and is used for sitting on tatami mats and floor.<br><br>Elasticity as much as Namba cushion is not required and the main purpose is to prevent body temperature from being deprived to the floor.<br><br>It was made around the Kamakura period, originally used as a symbol of power of authority and high priest.<br><br>It is said that middle people of the Edo period spread widely among ordinary people.<br><br>It is a design which was originally completed, and it is widely used even now. | A cloth cushion stuffed with cotton, used for sitting on tatami or the floor. It insulates the sitter from the cold floor. Once associated with dignitaries, it later became common in ordinary homes. | Un coussin de tissu garni de coton pour s'asseoir sur un tatami ou à même le sol. Il isole du froid du plancher. Autrefois réservé aux dignitaires, il s'est progressivement répandu dans les foyers. |  |
| `WA_Seat_Zabuton_Pink.label` | Zabuton(Pink) | Zabuton(Pink) | Zabuton rose |  |

| `WA_AlcoveA.label` | Alcove(Pine) | Alcove(Pine) | Alcôve au pin |  |
| `WA_AlcoveA.description` | Tokonoma is a recessed alcove in a Japanese-style<br>room. It is a place of honor where a hanging scroll,<br>vase of arranged flowers or other ornaments are <br>displayed. | Tokonoma is a recessed alcove in a Japanese-style<br>room. It is a place of honor where a hanging scroll,<br>vase of arranged flowers or other ornaments are <br>displayed. | Le tokonoma est une alcôve d'honneur dans une pièce japonaise. On y expose un rouleau calligraphié, un bouquet ou d'autres ornements. |  |

| `WA_Koto.label` | Koto | Koto | Koto |  |
| `WA_Koto.description` | Koto is a thirteen-stringed musical instrumentsimilar to a zither.The stirngs stretch over an oblong board. | A Japanese thirteen-stringed zither with strings stretched over a long wooden body. | Une cithare japonaise à treize cordes tendues sur une longue caisse de bois. |  |
| `WA_Karuta.label` | Karuta | Karuta | Karuta |  |
| `WA_Karuta.description` | Karuta is an indoor playground equipment that uses cards, mainly playing in the New Year. | A traditional Japanese card game, especially popular at New Year. | Un jeu de cartes traditionnel japonais, particulièrement apprécié au Nouvel An. |  |
| `WA_ShellMatching.label` | ShellMatching | Shell matching | Jeu de coquillages |  |
| `WA_ShellMatching.description` | Shell Matching is a Japanese game transmitted from the Heian period. It was a play of aristocrats who competed for coloring of seashells, beauty of shape, rarity, or singing songs with the shellfish as a theme and competing for superiority or inferiority. | A traditional game of matching shells, recalling courtly pastimes in which their shapes, colours and associated poetry were admired. | Un loisir traditionnel où l'on associe des coquillages. Il évoque les jeux de cour de l'époque de Heian, où l'on admirait leur forme et leurs couleurs et composait des poèmes. |  |
| `WA_Sugoroku.label` | Sugoroku | Sugoroku | Sugoroku |  |
| `WA_Sugoroku.description` | Sugoroku is a board game that advances the piece in the square according to the eyes that waved the dice and brings it closer. | A board game in which players move their pieces according to dice rolls. | Un jeu de plateau où les pions avancent selon le résultat des dés. |  |
| `WA_Cassolette.label` | Cassolette | Incense burner | Brûle-encens |  |
| `WA_Cassolette.description` | An Cassolette is used to heat a perfume in a solid state and emit fragrance components.The nobility of the Heian period was a routine thing for men and women to be able to wear kimonos into incense burners and smell their bodies and clothes with scents. | A vessel for heating solid incense to release its scent. At the Heian court, incense was also used to perfume clothing. | Un récipient servant à chauffer de l'encens solide pour en libérer le parfum. À la cour de Heian, on parfumait aussi les vêtements à la fumée d'encens. |  |
| `WA_Fusuma01_Akebono.label` | Fusuma-Dawn | Fusuma-Dawn | Fusuma à l'aube |  |
| `WA_Fusuma01_Akebono.description` | A sliding partition of paper or cloth stretched over a wooden frame. Light and quick to open, but easily damaged, it divides the rooms of a house. | A sliding partition of paper or cloth stretched over a wooden frame. Light and quick to open, but easily damaged, it divides the rooms of a house. | Une cloison coulissante en papier ou en tissu tendu sur un cadre de bois. Très légère, elle s'ouvre rapidement mais résiste mal aux chocs. Elle sert à séparer les pièces. |  |

| `WA_FoldingScreen.label` | Folding Screen | Folding Screen | Paravent |  |
| `WA_FoldingScreen.description` | Byobu is a folding screen which consists of a wooden frame covered with thick Japanese paper or cloth, usually silk.<br>It is used as a decorative screen or room devider. | A folding screen of wooden frames covered with thick Japanese paper or cloth, often silk. It decorates a room or divides the space. | Un byōbu est un paravent pliant composé de cadres de bois couverts de papier japonais épais ou de tissu, souvent de soie. Il décore et sépare les espaces. |  |
| `WA_Futon01m.description` | Futon is a thick bed quilt or mattress stuffed with cottonwool.A set of futon placed on Tatami mats is used as bedding. | A thick quilt or mattress stuffed with cotton. A futon laid on tatami serves as traditional bedding. | Un matelas ou une couverture épaisse garnie de coton. Posé sur des tatamis, le futon constitue une literie traditionnelle. |  |

| `WA_HFuton01m.description` | Futon is a thick bed quilt or mattress stuffed with cottonwool.A set of futon placed on Tatami mats is used as bedding.This futon is highly antimicrobial treated and can be used in medical practice. | A futon with an antimicrobial treatment, suitable for medical care. | Un futon ayant reçu un traitement antimicrobien, adapté aux soins médicaux. |  |

| `WA_Irori.label` | Irori | Irori | Irori |  |
| `WA_Irori.description` | It is a type of furnace permanently installed indoors.<br>It is a corner established in traditional Japanese houses to cut off the floor square, open ash, spread firewood and charcoal fire.<br>It is of the type put in the middle of a large room and has various roles such as heating, cooking, lighting, drying. | A sunken hearth in a square opening in the floor, filled with ash, wood and charcoal. It provides heating, cooking, light and a place to dry things. | Un foyer aménagé dans une ouverture carrée du plancher, rempli de cendres, de bois et de charbon. Au centre d'une pièce, il sert à chauffer, cuisiner, éclairer et sécher. |  |
| `WA_Chagama.label` | Irori(TeaSet) | Irori(TeaSet) | Irori pour le thé |  |
| `WA_Chagama.description` | It is a type of furnace permanently installed indoors.<br>It is a corner established in traditional Japanese houses to cut off the floor square, open ash, spread firewood and charcoal fire.<br>Most of the hearths used in the tea room are sized for one tatami mat, mainly used for boiling water. | A sunken hearth set into the floor of a tea room, primarily used to heat water for tea. | Un foyer aménagé dans le plancher d'une pièce de thé. Il sert principalement à chauffer l'eau de la cérémonie du thé. |  |
| `WA_Zabuton_Dummy.label` | ダミー座布団 | Auxiliary zabuton | Zabuton auxiliaire |  |
| `WA_Zabuton_Dummy.description` | ダミーです。 | An auxiliary hearth component. | Élément auxiliaire du foyer. |  |
| `WA_SutrasDesk.label` | sutra desk | sutra desk | Bureau de copie des sutras |  |
| `WA_SutrasDesk.description` | A low writing desk for copying sutras by hand. Slow, exacting work that doubles as a place to study. | A low writing desk for copying sutras by hand. Slow, exacting work that doubles as a place to study. | Un bureau bas pour recopier des sutras à la main. Un travail lent et minutieux, dans un espace qui sert aussi à étudier. |  |
| `WA_Wall_Kawara.label` | Rampart | Rampart | Rempart |  |
| `WA_Wall_Kawara.description` | It is a type of painted wall.<br>The roof tiles(Kawara) roofed on the plastics wall also combine rain removal and decoration.<br>At that time the tiles were expensive, mainly used for the outer walls of samurai residences. | A plastered wall capped with roof tiles for weather protection and decoration. Such walls once surrounded the residences of samurai. | Un mur enduit coiffé de tuiles. Ces tuiles le protègent de la pluie et le décorent ; autrefois coûteuses, elles ornaient notamment les enceintes des résidences de samouraïs. |  |
| `WA_Wall_Demado.label` | Rampart(bay window) | Rampart(bay window) | Rempart à fenêtre en saillie |  |
| `WA_Wall_Demado.description` | It is one with a bay window attached to the wall with tiles.<br>The bay window functioned as a surveillance window besides the role of giving aesthetic appearance during normal times.<br>In addition, it fulfills the role of firearms and stone drops at wartime. | A tiled wall with a projecting window for watching the surroundings and supporting its defenders. | Un mur coiffé de tuiles et doté d'une fenêtre en saillie. Celle-ci sert à observer les alentours et peut aider les défenseurs en cas d'attaque. |  |
| `WA_Wall_StoneWall.label` | Stone Wall | Stone Wall | Mur de pierre |  |
| `WA_Wall_StoneWall.description` | Ishigaki is a wall created by assembling stones and rocks, and is mainly used as a defense such as a castle.<br>Initially it was just a stack of natural stones, but as the era has progressed, things that increased the defensibility by loading stones machined without gaps became mainstream. | Ishigaki is a wall created by assembling stones and rocks, and is mainly used as a defense such as a castle.<br>Initially it was just a stack of natural stones, but as the era has progressed, things that increased the defensibility by loading stones machined without gaps became mainstream. | Un ishigaki est un mur de pierres assemblées, utilisé notamment dans les fortifications. Les ouvrages les plus élaborés emploient des pierres ajustées avec très peu d'interstices. |  |
| `WA_Wall_WaterMoats.label` | Water Moats | Water Moats | Douves en eau |  |
| `WA_Wall_WaterMoats.description` | The moat was dug around the castle and temple from the ancient times to prevent invasion of enemies and animals.<br>It has width and depth that can not easily be exceeded and it functions as an important defense facility.<br>Those water-trenched to the moat are called Mizuhori in particular, and have been incorporated into many castles since the modern era. | A wide, deep ditch dug around a fortification to slow intruders. Filled with water, it is a serious obstacle for attackers. | Un fossé large et profond, creusé autour d'une fortification pour freiner les intrusions. Rempli d'eau, il constitue un obstacle sérieux pour les assaillants. |  |
| `WA_Wall_DryMoats.label` | Dry Moats | Dry Moats | Douves sèches |  |
| `WA_Wall_DryMoats.description` | The moat was dug around the castle and temple from the ancient times to prevent invasion of enemies and animals.<br>It has width and depth that can not easily be exceeded and it functions as an important defense facility.<br>Those water-trenched to the moat are called Mizuhori in particular, and have been incorporated into many castles since the modern era. | A wide, deep ditch dug around a fortification to slow intruders. It stays dry and is an obstacle for attackers. | Un fossé large et profond, creusé autour d'une fortification pour freiner les intrusions. Il reste à sec et constitue un obstacle pour les assaillants. |  |
| `WA_Step.label` | Step | Step | Marche |  |
| `WA_Step.description` | The staircase is a structure to move to a place with a height difference.<br>It is divided into several horizontal stages with a height that can be raised and lowered by human feet.<br>Rimworld without the notion of step difference has no meaning other than appearance. | A decorative step suggesting a change in floor level. RimWorld does not model such changes, so its role here is purely visual. | Une marche décorative évoquant un escalier. RimWorld ne représentant pas les différences de niveau du sol, elle n'a ici qu'un rôle esthétique. |  |
| `WA_Gate_CastleGate.label` | Castle Gate | Castle Gate | Porte fortifiée |  |
| `WA_Gate_CastleGate.description` | The castle gate was one of the few places to enter the castle and it was one of the most important defense lines at wartime.<br>For that reason, it was easy to make a robust structure that can not be withdrawn, and a lot of ingenious efforts were made for interception. | A strong, fortified castle entrance designed to aid its defenders. | Une entrée fortifiée, solide et conçue pour faciliter la défense d'un château. |  |
| `WA_Gate_IronGate.label` | Castle Door | Castle Door | Porte de château |  |
| `WA_Gate_IronGate.description` | The castle gate was one of the few places to enter the castle and it was one of the most important defense lines at wartime.<br>Some of the gate is made of heavy iron, and even just opening it required a very large effort. | A heavy door protecting a castle entrance, sometimes reinforced with iron. | Une lourde porte protégeant l'entrée d'un château. Certaines portes historiques étaient renforcées de fer et difficiles à manœuvrer. |  |
| `WA_Fence_Yamatobei.label` | Yamato Fence | Yamato Fence | Clôture yamato |  |
| `WA_Fence_Yamatobei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Yamato-Fence is a fence stretched from the front and back with alternating plates one by one.<br>I open a gap by passing a crosspiece between the boards.<br>By doing so, it is a simple fence that blocks the line of sight and also passes the wind. | A fence with boards alternating on either side of its crosspieces. It blocks the view while allowing air to pass. | Une clôture dont les planches alternent de part et d'autre des traverses. Elle coupe la vue tout en laissant circuler l'air. |  |
| `WA_Fence_Sukibei.label` | Suki Fence | Suki Fence | Clôture suki |  |
| `WA_Fence_Sukibei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Suki-Fence is a covered fence, so that the interior can be seen through by placing a lattice in the middle.<br>It is mainly used in shrines and temples. | A covered fence with a central lattice that allows a partial view inside. Often used at shrines and temples. | Une clôture couverte comportant un treillis central qui laisse entrevoir l'intérieur. On la rencontre notamment dans les sanctuaires et les temples. |  |
| `WA_Fence_Tuijibei.label` | Tsuiji Fence | Tsuiji Fence | Clôture tsuiji |  |
| `WA_Fence_Tuijibei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Tsuiji-Fence sets up a framework by setting up a pillar on the foundation of stone wall, pouring and kneading the kneading soil.<br>It has been used in public places and temples since ancient times, and is still often built now. | A traditional wall of packed earth held by a wooden framework on a stone foundation. | Une clôture traditionnelle en terre pétrie maintenue par une ossature de bois sur un soubassement de pierre. Elle entoure depuis longtemps temples et édifices publics. |  |
| `WA_Wall_White.label` | Makabe(White) | Makabe(White) | Makabe blanc |  |
| `WA_Wall_White.description` | Creating traditional Japanese walls is called Makabe.<br>Makabe has a structure in which pillars and beams are exposed in the room, and it is said that durability is excellent because moisture easily escapes from the inside of the wall. | A traditional Japanese wall with exposed posts and beams. Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | Un mur japonais traditionnel dont les poteaux et poutres restent apparents. Cette structure permet à l'humidité de s'évacuer et favorise la durabilité du mur. |  |
| `WA_Wall_Pink.label` | Makabe(Pink) | Makabe(Pink) | Makabe rose |  |
| `WA_Wall_Pink.description` | Creating traditional Japanese walls is called Makabe.<br>Makabe has a structure in which pillars and beams are exposed in the room, and it is said that durability is excellent because moisture easily escapes from the inside of the wall. | A traditional Japanese wall with exposed posts and beams. Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | Un mur japonais traditionnel dont les poteaux et poutres restent apparents. Cette structure permet à l'humidité de s'évacuer et favorise la durabilité du mur. |  |

Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | Un mur japonais traditionnel dont les poteaux et poutres restent apparents. | `WA_Wall_Black.description` | Creating traditional Japanese walls is called Makabe.<br>Makabe has a structure in which pillars and beams are exposed in the room, and it is said that durability is excellent because moisture easily escapes from the inside of the wall. | A traditional Japanese wall with exposed posts and beams. Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | Un mur japonais traditionnel dont les poteaux et poutres restent apparents. Cette structure permet à l'humidité de s'évacuer et favorise la durabilité du mur. |  |
| `WA_GreenTea.label` | Green Tea | Green Tea | Thé vert |  |
| `WA_GreenTea.description` | Green Tea is a mixture of powdered tea leaves into hot water. | Green Tea is a mixture of powdered tea leaves into hot water. | Une boisson préparée en mélangeant des feuilles de thé en poudre à de l'eau chaude. |  |
| `WA_IchigoDaifuku.label` | Ichigo Daifuku | Ichigo Daifuku | Ichigo daifuku |  |
| `WA_IchigoDaifuku.description` | "Daifuku" is a rice cake stuffed with sweet filling like red bean paste.Daifuku literally means "great luck".<br>When eating, the fragrance of fresh strawberry spreads in the mouth, you can enjoy the sweetness of soft rice cake texture and elegant sweetness in exquisite balance. | A soft mochi filled with sweet red bean paste and a fresh strawberry. The fruit's tartness balances the sweet filling. | Un mochi garni de pâte de haricots rouges et d'une fraise fraîche. La douceur de la pâte accompagne l'acidité du fruit. |  |
| `WA_MitarashiDango.label` | Mitarashi Dango | Mitarashi Dango | Mitarashi dango |  |
| `WA_MitarashiDango.description` | "Mitarashi Dango" is a skewer dumpling with sugar and soy sauce.<br>The amber colored sweet taste is compatible with the rice flour dumplings, and the fragrant aroma caused by grilling with charcoal also enhances appetite. | Skewered rice-flour dumplings coated in a sweet soy-based sauce. Their grilled aroma is appetising. | Des boulettes de farine de riz en brochette, nappées d'une sauce sucrée à base de sauce soja. Leur parfum grillé ouvre l'appétit. |  |
| `WA_Geppei.label` | Geppei | Geppei | Geppei |  |
| `WA_Geppei.description` | "Geppei" is an old sweet which is eaten in Mid-Autumn Festival in China.<br>It has a round shape that is regarded as a full moon, wrapped in a soft dough and baked red bean paste mixed with walnuts, pine nuts etc. in soft cloth. | A round mooncake filled with sweet bean paste and nuts, traditionally associated with the Mid-Autumn Festival. | Un gâteau de lune traditionnel, garni de pâte de haricots et de fruits à coque. Sa forme ronde évoque la pleine lune de la fête de la mi-automne. |  |
| `WA_KashiwaMochi.label` | Kashiwa Mochi | Kashiwa Mochi | Kashiwa mochi |  |
| `WA_KashiwaMochi.description` | "Kashihachi" is wrapped in red bean paste with rice cake of rice flour. | A rice-flour mochi filled with sweet bean paste and wrapped in an oak leaf. | Un mochi à la pâte de haricots rouges, enveloppé d'une feuille de chêne. |  |
| `WA_Kasuteira.label` | Kasuteira | Kasuteira | Kasutera |  |
| `WA_Kasuteira.description` | "Kasuteira" is confections adopted from Portugal.<br>However, the actual situation is a completely different sweet that developed independently in Japan.<br>Kasuteira is made by burning a flour fabric mixed with plenty of sugar and eggs. | A light cake made from eggs, flour and sugar. Inspired by Portuguese baking, it developed its own distinctive form in Japan. | Un gâteau moelleux aux œufs, à la farine et au sucre. Inspiré de pâtisseries portugaises, il s'est développé au Japon sous une forme propre. |  |
| `WA_Kintuba.label` | Kintuba | Kintuba | Kintsuba |  |
| `WA_Kintuba.description` | "Kintuba" is a sweet made with red bean paste hardened with agar and baked with water flavored flour. | Sweet bean paste set with agar and cooked with a thin coating of batter. | Une pâtisserie à la pâte de haricots rouges prise à l'agar-agar et couverte d'une fine croûte de pâte cuite. |  |
| `WA_Nerikiri.label` | Nerikiri | Nerikiri | Nerikiri |  |
| `WA_Nerikiri.description` | "Nerikiri" is a confectionary refined and adjusted by adding a connecting ingredient to white bean paste. | A decorative sweet shaped from white bean paste mixed with a binder, often coloured and formed into delicate patterns. | Une pâtisserie façonnée à partir de pâte de haricots blancs et d'un liant, souvent travaillée en motifs colorés. |  |
| `WA_SakuraNUguisu.label` | Sakura and Uguisu Mochi | Sakura and Uguisu Mochi | Sakura et uguisu mochi |  |
| `WA_SakuraNUguisu.description` | It is a sweet imitating the color of cherry blossoms and the color of young plants. | Sweets evoking cherry blossoms and fresh spring shoots. | Des douceurs évoquant les couleurs des fleurs de cerisier et des jeunes pousses. |  |
| `WA_Youkan.label` | Youkan | Youkan | Yōkan |  |
| `WA_Youkan.description` | "Youkan" is a Japanese sweet made by pouring azuki bean paste in mold and solidified with agar. | A Japanese sweet made from azuki bean paste, moulded and set with agar. | Une confiserie japonaise de pâte de haricots azuki, moulée et prise à l'agar-agar. |  |
| `RPC_Dirt.label` | soil | soil | Terre |  |
| `RPC_Dirt.description` | Packed earth, dug out and carried in. Mixed with straw and left to dry it makes the daub of a wattle-and-daub wall. | Packed earth, dug out and carried in. Mixed with straw and left to dry it makes the daub of a wattle-and-daub wall. | De la terre compacte, creusée et transportée. Mélangée à de la paille puis séchée, elle sert à fabriquer du torchis. |  |
| `RPC_Charcoal.label` | charcoal | charcoal | Charbon de bois |  |
| `RPC_Charcoal.description` | Wood burned slowly in a starved fire until only carbon is left. It burns hot, steady and nearly smokeless, which is what makes it fit to burn indoors. | Wood burned slowly in a starved fire until only carbon is left. It burns hot, steady and nearly smokeless, which is what makes it fit to burn indoors. | Du bois lentement carbonisé dans un feu pauvre en air. Il brûle avec une chaleur régulière et peu de fumée, ce qui le rend utile dans les foyers intérieurs. |  |
| `RPC_JapanesePaper.label` | washi | washi | Washi |  |
| `RPC_JapanesePaper.description` | Paper combed from the long bark fibres of the kozo shrub. It is far tougher than it looks: stretched over a light wooden frame it holds up as a wall. | Paper combed from the long bark fibres of the kozo shrub. It is far tougher than it looks: stretched over a light wooden frame it holds up as a wall. | Du papier fabriqué avec les longues fibres d'écorce du kōzo. Résistant malgré sa finesse, il peut couvrir une armature légère pour former une cloison. |  |
| `RPC_PureSilk.label` | pure silk | pure silk | Soie pure |  |
| `RPC_PureSilk.description` | Thread reeled off silkworm cocoons and woven, with nothing else blended in. Light, strong and cool against the skin, and slow enough to make that a bolt of it is worth its weight. | Thread reeled off silkworm cocoons and woven, with nothing else blended in. Light, strong and cool against the skin, and slow enough to make that a bolt of it is worth its weight. | Du fil dévidé de cocons de vers à soie puis tissé sans mélange. Sa finesse, sa solidité et sa fabrication lente en font une étoffe précieuse. |  |
| `WA_Azuki.label` | Azuki | Azuki | Azuki |  |
| `WA_Azuki.description` | Raw Red beens. | Raw red beans. | Haricots rouges crus. |  |
| `WA_TeaLeaf.label` | TeaLeaf | Tea leaves | Feuilles de thé |  |
| `WA_TeaLeaf.description` | Raw Tea Leaf. | Fresh tea leaves. | Feuilles de thé fraîches. |  |
| `WA_Igusa.label` | Rush | Rush | Jonc |  |
| `WA_Igusa.description` | Rush is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | Rush is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | Une plante utilisée pour fabriquer des tatamis, et autrefois employée dans certains remèdes. |  |
| `RPC_Cocoon.label` | Cocoon | Cocoon | Cocon |  |
| `RPC_Cocoon.description` | It is the cocoon made by silkworm larvae.<br>Weaving the thread by spinning it will become the finest textile. | A silkworm cocoon. Its thread can be reeled off to produce fine silk fabric. | Un cocon de ver à soie. On en dévide le fil pour produire une étoffe fine. |  |
| `RPC_Kouzo.label` | Kouzo | Kouzo | Kōzo |  |
| `RPC_Kouzo.description` | It is a bundle of bark which took from Kouzo Tree.<br>Fibers of Kouzo bark are strongly intertwined long.<br>Paper made from Kouzo is strong and lasts long. | A bundle of paper-mulberry bark. Its long, intertwined fibres make strong, lasting paper. | Un faisceau d'écorce de mûrier à papier. Ses longues fibres entrelacées permettent de fabriquer un papier solide et durable. |  |

| `WA_Dressing_Sumoman.label` | Kimono-Rikishi | Kimono-Rikishi | Kimono de rikishi |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Sumoman.description` | Kimono of Rikishi.A Yokozuna with a mighty power overwhelms the enemy. | A wrestler's kimono. The yokozuna's imposing presence intimidates enemies. | Un kimono de lutteur. La puissance du yokozuna intimide les ennemis. |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Igusa.label` | Rush | Rush | Jonc |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Igusa.description` | It is an elongated plant inhabited by wetlands and large ones grow to about 1.6 m.<br>It is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | A tall wetland rush used primarily to make tatami mats, and historically in some herbal remedies. | Un jonc allongé des zones humides, pouvant atteindre environ 1,6 mètre. Il sert surtout à fabriquer des tatamis et était autrefois utilisé comme plante médicinale. |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Azuki.label` | Azuki | Azuki | Azuki |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Azuki.description` | Tie the dark red beans to the sheath.<br>Beans are cooked or boiled sweet and then made into bean paste. | A plant bearing pods of dark red beans. These can be cooked as food or sweetened into bean paste. | Une plante donnant des gousses de haricots rouge sombre. Ceux-ci se mangent cuits ou sucrés sous forme de pâte. |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_TeaTree.label` | TeaTree | TeaTree | Théier |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_TeaTree.description` | It is said that cultivation began in China since BC, it is used not only for edible but also for appreciation.<br>The tea extracted from the leaves with water is loved around the world. | A shrub long cultivated for its leaves, which can be infused to make tea. | Un arbuste cultivé depuis longtemps en Chine. Ses feuilles infusées donnent une boisson appréciée dans le monde entier. |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kouzo.label` | Kouzo | Kouzo | Kōzo |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kouzo.description` | In spring, light yellow green flowers bloom and red fruits in early summer.<br>It is used as a raw material for paper because it can take high quality fibers from the bark. | A paper-mulberry tree whose bark provides strong fibres for papermaking. | Un arbre aux fleurs vert pâle au printemps et aux fruits rouges en début d'été. Les fibres de son écorce servent à fabriquer du papier. |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kuwa.label` | Kuwa | Kuwa | Mûrier |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kuwa.description` | Mulberry ties the purple of purplish red purple.<br>It is used as a tree for silkworms since ancient times, it is a plant indispensable for making silk. | A mulberry tree with purplish-red fruit. Its leaves feed silkworms, making it important for silk production. | Un mûrier aux fruits rouge violacé. Ses feuilles nourrissent les vers à soie, ce qui en fait une plante essentielle à la production de soie. |  |

| `WA_Hediff_Nerikiri.stages.Multicolored_pastemaking_is_artistic.label` | Multicolored paste-making is artistic! | These colourful sweets are little works of art! | 色とりどりの練り切りは芸術的だ！ |  |

| `WA_Hediff_Sakura.stages.An_elegant_scent_of_cherry_leaves_and_a_fabric_of_beautiful_color_make_spring_feel.label` | An elegant scent of cherry leaves and a fabric of beautiful color make spring feel. | The scent of cherry leaves and these delicate colours feel like spring. | 桜の葉の上品な香りと美しい色の生地が春を感じさせる。 |  |

| `WA_Recipe_GreenTea.label` | Make Green Tea | Make Green Tea | 抹茶を点てる |  |
| `WA_Recipe_GreenTea.description` | Dissolve tea leaves in powdered form in hot water and sprinkle tea. | Whisk powdered tea leaves into hot water. | 茶の葉を粉末状にしたものを湯に溶かし、お茶を点てます。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_GreenTea.jobString` | Making Green Tea | Making Green Tea | 抹茶を点てている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Daifuku.label` | Make Ichigo Daifuku | Make Ichigo Daifuku | 苺大福を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Daifuku.description` | Make big strawberries wrapped in bean paste and rice cake. | Wrap strawberries in sweet bean paste and mochi. | 大粒の苺を餡と餅で包んで作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Daifuku.jobString` | Making Ichigo Daifuku. | Making Ichigo Daifuku. | 苺大福を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Dango.label` | Make Mitarashi dumplings | Make Mitarashi dumplings | みたらし団子を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Dango.description` | Make rice flour dumplings with a sweet amber colored sauce. | Make rice flour dumplings with a sweet amber colored sauce. | 米粉で作った団子に甘辛い琥珀色のソースをかけて作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Dango.jobString` | Making Mitarashi dumplings. | Making Mitarashi dumplings. | みたらし団子を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Geppei.label` | Make Geppei | Make Geppei | 月餅を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Geppei.description` | Wrap the red bean paste in a dough and bake it. | Wrap sweet bean paste in pastry and bake it. | 小豆餡を生地で包んで焼きます。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Geppei.jobString` | Making Geppei. | Making Geppei. | 月餅を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kashiwa.label` | Make Kashiwa Mochi | Make Kashiwa Mochi | 柏餅を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kashiwa.description` | Wrapped in red bean paste with rice cake, wrapped in leaves of bamboo to make. | Wrap sweet bean paste in mochi, then wrap the cake in an oak leaf. | 小豆餡を餅で包み、槲の葉で包んで作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kashiwa.jobString` | Making Kashiwa Mochi. | Making Kashiwa Mochi. | 柏餅を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kasuteira.label` | Make Kasuteira | Make Kasuteira | かすていらを作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kasuteira.description` | Bubble the egg whites and mix the flour and sugar mixed dough. | Mix beaten eggs, flour and sugar into a light cake batter. | 卵白を泡立てて小麦粉、砂糖を混ぜ合わせた生地を焼きます。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kasuteira.jobString` | Making Kasuteira. | Making Kasuteira. | かすていらを作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kintuba.label` | Make Kintuba | Make Kintuba | きんつばを作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kintuba.description` | Stir the grain bean paste with agar, bake each side with flour which is dissolved with water. | Set sweet bean paste with agar, then coat and cook each face in a thin batter. | 粒餡を寒天で固め、各面を水で溶いた小麦粉をつけて焼き上げます。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Kintuba.jobString` | Making Kintuba. | Making Kintuba. | きんつばを作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Nerikiri.label` | Make Nerikiri | Make Nerikiri | 練り切りを作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Nerikiri.description` | Add ingredients for the tie to the tinted bean paste, adjust and make it by making it. | Mix a binder into coloured bean paste and shape it into decorative sweets. | 色合いをつけた餡につなぎの食材を加え、調整して練りあげて作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Nerikiri.jobString` | Making Nerikiri. | Making Nerikiri. | 練り切りを作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_SakuraNUguisu.label` | Make Sakura Mochi and Uguisu Mochi | Make Sakura Mochi and Uguisu Mochi | 桜餅と鶯餅を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_SakuraNUguisu.description` | I make wrapped bean paste with two types of leather that colors spring. | Wrap sweet bean paste in two kinds of colourful spring mochi. | 春を彩る2種類の皮で餡を包んで作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_SakuraNUguisu.jobString` | Making Sakura Mochi and Uguisu Mochi. | Making Sakura Mochi and Uguisu Mochi. | 桜餅と鶯餅を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Youkan.label` | Make Youkan | Make Youkan | 羊羹を作る |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Youkan.description` | Add the agar to the red bean paste and boil down to make it. | Cook sweet bean paste with agar to make a firm sweet. | 小豆餡に寒天を加えて煮詰めて作ります。 |  |
| `WA_Recipe_Youkan.jobString` | Making Youkan. | Making Youkan. | 羊羹を作っている。 |  |
| `WA_Igusa2Medicine.label` | Purification Medicine | Prepare herbal medicine | 調薬する |  |
| `WA_Igusa2Medicine.description` | Purify rush as MedicineHerbals. | Process rush into herbal medicine. | イ草を煎じて薬にします。 |  |
| `WA_Igusa2Medicine.jobString` | Purify rush. | Preparing rush medicine. | イ草を煎じている。 |  |
| `RPC_MakePureSilk.label` | Weaving silk | Weaving silk | 生糸を紡ぐ |  |
| `RPC_MakePureSilk.description` | Weaving Pure Silk from cocoon. | Weave pure silk from silkworm cocoons. | 生糸を紡いで織り込んでいます。 |  |
| `RPC_MakePureSilk.jobString` | Weaving silk. | Weaving silk. | 生糸を紡いでいる。 |  |
| `RPC_MakePaper.label` | Combing the Japanese paper | Make Japanese paper | 楮を漉く |  |
| `RPC_MakePaper.description` | Combing the Japanese paper from Kouzo | Make Japanese paper from kozo bark fibres. | 楮を漉いて和紙を作ります。 |  |
| `RPC_MakePaper.jobString` | Combing paper. | Making paper. | 楮を漉いている。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ResearchProjectDef/Lang_WA-ResearchProject.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Research_Basic.label` | WA Basic | WA Basic | 和風技術_基礎 |  |
| `WA_Research_Basic.description` | Learn the basics of Japanese technology. We will be able to study various Japanese technologies. | Learn the foundations of Japanese techniques to unlock further research. | 日本技術の基礎を学びます。各種日本技術を研究できるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Basic.label` | Building Basic | Basic architecture | 和風建築技術_基礎 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Basic.description` | Learn the basics of Japanese style building Technology.We will be able to build basic walls and fences | Learn basic Japanese building techniques to construct simple walls and fences. | 和風建築の基礎を研究します。基本的な壁や塀を建築できるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Advanced.label` | Building Advanced | Advanced architecture | 和風建築技術_応用 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Advanced.description` | Learn the application of Japanese style building Technology.We will be able to build various walls and fences. | Learn advanced Japanese building techniques to construct a wider range of walls and fences. | 和風建築の応用を研究します。様々な壁や塀を建築できるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Castle.label` | Building Castle | Fortified architecture | 和風築城技術 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Castle.description` | Learn the Japanese style casting technology.We will be able to build Robust building for defense. | Learn Japanese fortification techniques to build strong defensive structures. | 和風築城技術を研究します。防衛向けの堅牢な建築ができるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Garden.label` | Gardening | Gardening | 和風造園技術 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Garden.description` | Learn the Japanese style gardening technology.We will be able to build the landscape. | Learn Japanese gardening techniques to shape the landscape. | 和風造園技術を研究します。景観を整える建築ができるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Funiture.label` | Funiture | Furniture | 和風家具技術 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Funiture.description` | Learn the Japanese style funiture technology.We will be able to build various furniture. | Learn to make Japanese-style furniture. | 和風家具の作成方法を研究します。様々な家具を製造できるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Amusement.label` | Amusement | Amusement | 和風娯楽 |  |
| `WA_Research_Building_Amusement.description` | Learn the Japanese style Entertainment technology.We will be able to build some entertainment supplies. | Learn to make equipment for traditional Japanese pastimes. | エンターテイメント技術を学びます。いくつかのエンターテイメント用品を作ることができるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Art.label` | Art | Art | 和風芸術品 |  |
| `WA_Research_Art.description` | Learn art.You will be able to make beautiful art pieces. | Learn to create beautiful works of art. | 芸術を学びます。美しい芸術品を作ることができるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Farming.label` | Farming | Farming | 和風植物 |  |
| `WA_Research_Farming.description` | Learn cultivation of plants indispensable for Japanese style living. | Learn cultivation of plants indispensable for Japanese style living. | 日常生活に欠かせない植物の栽培方法を学びます。 |  |
| `WA_Research_TeaTimeset.label` | Tea Time | Tea Time | 茶会 |  |
| `WA_Research_TeaTimeset.description` | Learn how to make tea.We will be able to make tea and kinds of tea confectionery. | Learn to prepare tea and traditional sweets to accompany it. | お茶の淹れ方を学びます。お茶と様々なお茶菓子の作成ができるようになります。 |  |
| `WA_Research_Dressing.label` | Dress up | Dress up | 和装 |  |
| `WA_Research_Dressing.description` | Learn how to make daily clothing. | Learn how to make daily clothing. | 日常的に使用する衣類の作成方法を学びます。 |  |

It is a place of honor where a hanging scroll,<br>vase of arranged flowers or other ornaments are <br>displayed. | Tokonoma is a recessed alcove in a Japanese-style<br>room. It is a place of honor where a hanging scroll,<br>vase of arranged flowers or other ornaments are <br>displayed. | 床の間とは日本式の部屋の中にあり英語ではrecessed alcoveと言います。そこは、掛け軸や花が活けられた花瓶やその他の装飾品が飾られた上座になります。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-Amusement.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Koto.label` | Koto | Koto | 箏 |  |
| `WA_Koto.description` | Koto is a thirteen-stringed musical instrumentsimilar to a zither.The stirngs stretch over an oblong board. | A Japanese thirteen-stringed zither with strings stretched over a long wooden body. | 箏は、弦楽器に似た13本の弦を持つ弦楽器です。弦は長方形の板の縦方向に張られています。 |  |
| `WA_Karuta.label` | Karuta | Karuta | かるた |  |
| `WA_Karuta.description` | Karuta is an indoor playground equipment that uses cards, mainly playing in the New Year. | A traditional Japanese card game, especially popular at New Year. | かるたは主に正月に遊ぶ、カードを使った室内遊具です。 |  |
| `WA_ShellMatching.label` | ShellMatching | Shell matching | 貝合わせ |  |
| `WA_ShellMatching.description` | Shell Matching is a Japanese game transmitted from the Heian period. It was a play of aristocrats who competed for coloring of seashells, beauty of shape, rarity, or singing songs with the shellfish as a theme and competing for superiority or inferiority. | A traditional game of matching shells, recalling courtly pastimes in which their shapes, colours and associated poetry were admired. | 貝合わせは、平安時代から伝わる日本の遊びです。貝殻の色合いや形の美しさ、珍しさを競ったり、その貝を題材にした歌を詠んでその優劣を競い合ったりする貴族たちの遊びでした。 |  |
| `WA_Sugoroku.label` | Sugoroku | Sugoroku | 双六 |  |
| `WA_Sugoroku.description` | Sugoroku is a board game that advances the piece in the square according to the eyes that waved the dice and brings it closer. | A board game in which players move their pieces according to dice rolls. | すごろくは、サイコロを振って出た目に従って升目にある駒を進めて上がりに近づけるボードゲームです。 |  |
| `WA_Cassolette.label` | Cassolette | Incense burner | 香炉 |  |
| `WA_Cassolette.description` | An Cassolette is used to heat a perfume in a solid state and emit fragrance components.The nobility of the Heian period was a routine thing for men and women to be able to wear kimonos into incense burners and smell their bodies and clothes with scents. | A vessel for heating solid incense to release its scent. At the Heian court, incense was also used to perfume clothing. | 香炉は固体状の香料を加熱し、香気成分を発散させる目的で用いる器です。平安時代の貴族は、着物を香炉にくゆらせて体や服に香りを付けて香りを纏うことが男女の差なく日常的なことでした。 |  |
| `WA_Aquarium.label` | Aquarium | Aquarium | 金魚鉢 |  |
| `WA_Aquarium.description` | As it became possible to produce colorless glass at the end of the Edo era, glass aquariums became widely spread among the commoners.<br>The aquarium containing the goldfish makes the viewer cool. | As it became possible to produce colorless glass at the end of the Edo era, glass aquariums became widely spread among the commoners.<br>The aquarium containing the goldfish makes the viewer cool. | 江戸時代後期になると無色ガラスが生産できるようになり、ガラス製の水槽が庶民にも広まるようになりました。<br>金魚を入れた水槽は見る人を涼しく感じさせます。 |  |

| `WA_Fusuma01_Akebono.description` | A sliding partition of paper or cloth stretched over a wooden frame. | `WA_Shoji01_Square.description` | A light wooden lattice covered with translucent paper. It admits soft light while screening the view. Its fragile paper is easily punctured. | A light wooden lattice covered with translucent paper. It admits soft light while screening the view. Its fragile paper is easily punctured. | 障子は格子状の骨組みに薄紙を張って作ります。ほのかに明かりを取り入れることができます。<br>とても軽いため、素早く開閉ができますが、耐久性は低いです。<br>現代の障子と同じ形のものが現れたのは鎌倉時代以前の平清盛のころと言われています。<br>非常に破れやすい構造であるため、ペットや子供のいる家では障子に穴が開くことは日常茶飯事です。 |  |
| `WA_Shoji02_Circle.description` | A light wooden lattice covered with translucent paper. It admits soft light while screening the view. | `WA_Dressing_Doctor.description` | Kimono of Doctor. | Kimono of Doctor. | 医者の服です。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_TeaMaster.label` | Kimono-TeaMaster | Kimono-TeaMaster | 和服-茶人 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_TeaMaster.description` | Kimono of Tea Master. | Kimono of Tea Master. | 茶人の服です。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Oiran.label` | Kimono-Oiran | Kimono-Oiran | 和服-花魁 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Oiran.description` | Kimono of Oiran. | Kimono of Oiran. | 花魁の服です。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Kamishimo.label` | Kimono-Kamishimo | Kimono-Kamishimo | 和服-裃 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Kamishimo.description` | Kimono of Kamishimo. | Kimono of Kamishimo. | 裃です。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Matagi.label` | Kimono-Matagi | Kimono-Matagi | 和服-マタギ |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Matagi.description` | Kimono of Matagi | Kimono of Matagi | マタギの服です。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Princess.label` | Kimono-Princess | Kimono-Princess | 和服-姫 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Princess.description` | Kimono of Princess.Princess' s dignity encourages surrounding friends. | A princess's kimono. Her dignity encourages nearby allies. | 姫の服です。姫の品格が周囲の味方を激励します。 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Sumoman.label` | Kimono-Rikishi | Kimono-Rikishi | 和服-力士 |  |
| `WA_Dressing_Sumoman.description` | Kimono of Rikishi.A Yokozuna with a mighty power overwhelms the enemy. | A wrestler's kimono. The yokozuna's imposing presence intimidates enemies. | 力士の服です。強大な力を持つ横綱の気迫は敵対するものを圧倒します。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-FarmPlant.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_FarmPlant_Azuki.label` | Azuki | Azuki | 小豆 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Azuki.description` | Tie the dark red beans to the sheath.<br>Beans are cooked or boiled sweet and then made into bean paste. | A plant bearing pods of dark red beans. These can be cooked as food or sweetened into bean paste. | 鞘に暗赤色の豆を結びます。<br>豆は煮込み料理にしたり、甘く煮詰めて餡にしたりします。 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_TeaTree.label` | TeaTree | TeaTree | 茶の木 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_TeaTree.description` | It is said that cultivation began in China since BC, it is used not only for edible but also for appreciation.<br>The tea extracted from the leaves with water is loved around the world. | A shrub long cultivated for its leaves, which can be infused to make tea. | 紀元前より中国で栽培が始まったといわれ、食用だけでなく鑑賞用としても用いられます。<br>葉から水で抽出したお茶は世界各地で愛されています。 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Igusa.label` | Rush | Rush | イ草 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Igusa.description` | It is an elongated plant inhabited by wetlands and large ones grow to about 1.6 m.<br>It is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | A tall wetland rush used primarily to make tatami mats, and historically in some herbal remedies. | 湿地に生息する細長い植物で、大きいものは1.6mほどまで成長します。<br>主に畳の原材料として利用されますが、古くは薬草として用いられていました。 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kouzo.label` | Kouzo | Kouzo | 楮 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kouzo.description` | In spring, light yellow green flowers bloom and red fruits in early summer.<br>It is used as a raw material for paper because it can take high quality fibers from the bark. | A paper-mulberry tree whose bark provides strong fibres for papermaking. | 楮は春に淡黄緑色の花が咲き、初夏に赤い実を結びます。<br>樹皮から良質な繊維が取れるため、紙の原料として使われています。 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kuwa.label` | Kuwa | Kuwa | 桑 |  |
| `WA_FarmPlant_Kuwa.description` | Mulberry ties the purple of purplish red purple.<br>It is used as a tree for silkworms since ancient times, it is a plant indispensable for making silk. | A mulberry tree with purplish-red fruit. Its leaves feed silkworms, making it important for silk production. | 桑は赤紫のノイチゴ状の実を結びます。<br>古くから養蚕用の樹木として利用されており、絹の作成には欠かせない植物です。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-FoldingScreen.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_FoldingScreen.label` | Folding Screen | Folding Screen | 屏風 |  |
| `WA_FoldingScreen.description` | Byobu is a folding screen which consists of a wooden frame covered with thick Japanese paper or cloth, usually silk.<br>It is used as a decorative screen or room devider. | A folding screen of wooden frames covered with thick Japanese paper or cloth, often silk. It decorates a room or divides the space. | 屏風は英語でa folding screenと言い、厚い和紙や絹などの布で覆われた木枠でできています。それは、装飾用のつい立として、または、部屋の間仕切りに使われます。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-Food.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_GreenTea.label` | Green Tea | Green Tea | 抹茶 |  |
| `WA_GreenTea.description` | Green Tea is a mixture of powdered tea leaves into hot water. | Green Tea is a mixture of powdered tea leaves into hot water. | 日本ではチャノキの葉から作った茶の多くは緑茶に分類されており、日本で最も飲まれている茶です。<br>茶の湯の席では抹茶を用いますが、抹茶も緑茶の一種です。<br>抹茶は茶葉を粉末にし、湯を加えて攪拌して飲料にします。 |  |
| `WA_IchigoDaifuku.label` | Ichigo Daifuku | Ichigo Daifuku | 苺大福 |  |
| `WA_IchigoDaifuku.description` | "Daifuku" is a rice cake stuffed with sweet filling like red bean paste.Daifuku literally means "great luck".<br>When eating, the fragrance of fresh strawberry spreads in the mouth, you can enjoy the sweetness of soft rice cake texture and elegant sweetness in exquisite balance. | A soft mochi filled with sweet red bean paste and a fresh strawberry. The fruit's tartness balances the sweet filling. | 大福は餡子のような甘い詰め物で詰まったお餅です。大福は文字通り「幸運」を意味します。<br>食べると口の中で新鮮な苺の香りが広がり、柔らかな餅の食感と上品な甘さの餡が絶妙なバランスで楽しめます。 |  |
| `WA_MitarashiDango.label` | Mitarashi Dango | Mitarashi Dango | みたらし団子 |  |
| `WA_MitarashiDango.description` | "Mitarashi Dango" is a skewer dumpling with sugar and soy sauce.<br>The amber colored sweet taste is compatible with the rice flour dumplings, and the fragrant aroma caused by grilling with charcoal also enhances appetite. | Skewered rice-flour dumplings coated in a sweet soy-based sauce. Their grilled aroma is appetising. | みたらし団子は砂糖醤油の葛餡をかけた串団子です。<br>琥珀色の甘辛のたれは米粉の団子と相性がよく、炭火で炙ったことによる香ばしさも食欲を引き立てます。 |  |
| `WA_Geppei.label` | Geppei | Geppei | 月餅 |  |
| `WA_Geppei.description` | "Geppei" is an old sweet which is eaten in Mid-Autumn Festival in China.<br>It has a round shape that is regarded as a full moon, wrapped in a soft dough and baked red bean paste mixed with walnuts, pine nuts etc. in soft cloth. | A round mooncake filled with sweet bean paste and nuts, traditionally associated with the Mid-Autumn Festival. | 月餅は中国で中秋節に食べられている、古くからあるお菓子です。<br>満月に見立てた丸い形をしており、胡桃や松の実などを混ぜた小豆餡を柔らかい生地で包んで焼きます。 |  |
| `WA_KashiwaMochi.label` | Kashiwa Mochi | Kashiwa Mochi | 柏餅 |  |
| `WA_KashiwaMochi.description` | "Kashihachi" is wrapped in red bean paste with rice cake of rice flour. | A rice-flour mochi filled with sweet bean paste and wrapped in an oak leaf. | 柏餅は上新粉の餅に小豆餡を入れ、槲の葉で包んだお菓子です。<br>もちもちとしていながら歯切れがよく、かみしめると米の優しい甘みがじわりと口の中に広がります。 |  |
| `WA_Kasuteira.label` | Kasuteira | Kasuteira | かすていら |  |
| `WA_Kasuteira.description` | "Kasuteira" is confections adopted from Portugal.<br>However, the actual situation is a completely different sweet that developed independently in Japan.<br>Kasuteira is made by burning a flour fabric mixed with plenty of sugar and eggs. | A light cake made from eggs, flour and sugar. Inspired by Portuguese baking, it developed its own distinctive form in Japan. | かすていらはポルトガルから伝わった南蛮菓子とされますが、実態は全くの別物で日本で独自に発展したお菓子です。<br>砂糖と卵をふんだんに使った生地は、しっとりしつつもふわりとした食感で、ザラメ生地の香ばしさも相まって贅沢な逸品となります。 |  |
| `WA_Kintuba.label` | Kintuba | Kintuba | きんつば |  |
| `WA_Kintuba.description` | "Kintuba" is a sweet made with red bean paste hardened with agar and baked with water flavored flour. | Sweet bean paste set with agar and cooked with a thin coating of batter. | きんつばは小豆餡を寒天で固めたものを水で溶いた小麦粉をつけて焼いて作るお菓子です。<br>艶やかでふっくらとした粒餡はみずみずしい食感を残しており、小豆の風味が生きた餡をたっぷり味わうことができます。 |  |
| `WA_Nerikiri.label` | Nerikiri | Nerikiri | 練り切り |  |
| `WA_Nerikiri.description` | "Nerikiri" is a confectionary refined and adjusted by adding a connecting ingredient to white bean paste. | A decorative sweet shaped from white bean paste mixed with a binder, often coloured and formed into delicate patterns. | 練り切りは白餡につなぎの食材を加えて調整して練り上げた生菓子です。<br>上生菓子と言われるほど美しく、味わいも豊であるため高級な和菓子とされます。<br>季節の移ろいを先取りしたデザインは、見るものの心を捉えて離しません。 |  |
| `WA_SakuraNUguisu.label` | Sakura and Uguisu Mochi | Sakura and Uguisu Mochi | 桜餅と鶯餅 |  |
| `WA_SakuraNUguisu.description` | It is a sweet imitating the color of cherry blossoms and the color of young plants. | Sweets evoking cherry blossoms and fresh spring shoots. | 春を知らせる桜の色と若草の色を模したお菓子です。<br>どちらも餡子をお米の生地で包んで作ります。<br>桜餅を包む桜の葉の香りと鶯餅にかけるきな粉の香りも幸福感をもたらします。 |  |
| `WA_Youkan.label` | Youkan | Youkan | 羊羹 |  |
| `WA_Youkan.description` | "Youkan" is a Japanese sweet made by pouring azuki bean paste in mold and solidified with agar. | A Japanese sweet made from azuki bean paste, moulded and set with agar. | 羊羹は小豆餡を型に流し込み寒天で固めた和菓子です。<br>糖度の高い羊羹は切り口から砂糖が析出するため、日がたつと外側が白く固まります。<br>この析出部分のシャリシャリ感と羊羹内部の柔らかさのハーモニーはまさに至高です。 |  |

| `WA_MFuton01m.description` | Futon is a thick bed quilt or mattress stuffed with cottonwool.A set of futon placed on Tatami mats is used as bedding.This futon is made for carrying and can be used like a bed roll. | A cotton-filled futon designed for carrying and use as a portable bedroll. | 布団は厚いベッドキルトまたは綿が詰められたマットレスのことです。畳の上に敷かれる一組の布団は寝具として使われます。この布団は持ち運び用に作られており、ベッドロールのように使用することが可能です。 |  |

| `WA_MFuton02m.description` | Futon is a thick bed quilt or mattress stuffed with cottonwool.A set of futon placed on Tatami mats is used as bedding.This futon is made for carrying and can be used like a bed roll. | A cotton-filled futon designed for carrying and use as a portable bedroll. | 布団は厚いベッドキルトまたは綿が詰められたマットレスのことです。畳の上に敷かれる一組の布団は寝具として使われます。この布団は持ち運び用に作られており、ベッドロールのように使用することが可能です。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-Glow.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Glow_01Toudai.label` | Cow dung lighthouse | Oil lamp stand | 牛糞灯台 |  |
| `WA_Glow_01Toudai.description` | The lighthouse lights the light with a light on the lamp core soaked in "kerosene" produced from the animal's oil.<br>It is said that it began to be used around the Heian era, and it is said that the aristocratic group used to love mainly.<br>Cow dung lighthouse has been called by this name because its base part resembles the form of cow dung. | An oil lamp with a wick resting in a shallow dish. Its traditional name refers to the shape of its base, which resembles cow dung. Such lamps were once used in aristocratic households. | 灯台は動物の油から作り出した「灯油」に浸した灯芯に火をつけて明かりを灯します。平安時代頃から使われ始めたといわれており、主に貴族階級が愛用していたといわれています。牛糞灯台は土台部分が牛糞の形に似ていることからこの名前で呼ばれるようになりました。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_02Gatou.label` | Gato | Gato | 瓦灯 |  |
| `WA_Glow_02Gatou.description` | Gato is also a lighting fixture made from "kerosene" made from animal fat.<br>It began to be used around the Muromachi period, and it circulated enough to be used as a light source for the common people in the Edo period.<br>It is called a tile light because it makes it by baking it using the same soil as the Kawara.<br>It is one of the popular reasons to be flame retardant materials. | A clay oil lamp made from material similar to roof tiles. Its fire-resistant construction helped it become a common household light. | 瓦灯もまた動物の脂から作った「灯油」を燃料にした照明器具です。室町時代頃から使われ始め、江戸時代には庶民のあかりとして使われるほどに流通しました。瓦と同じ土を使って焼き上げて作るため、瓦灯と呼ばれています。難燃性の素材であることも人気の理由の一つです。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_03Andon.label` | Ariake lantern | Ariake lantern | 有明行灯 |  |
| `WA_Glow_03Andon.description` | Lighthouses and Gato were problematic in windshields, so lanterns wrapped in Japanese paper were invented.<br>Lanterns were originally used for carrying, but since it was "kerosene" made from animal oil, there is a difficulty in portability and it will be used for indoor stationary use.<br>Ariake lanterns can be illuminated brightly when used out from the box, and when used in a box it gained popularity as an excellent one that can be used as a bedtime light with suppressed brightness. | A paper-covered lantern that shelters its flame. Outside its box it gives a brighter light; inside, it becomes a subdued night light. | 灯台や瓦灯は風防に問題があったため、明かりを和紙で包む行灯が発明されました。行灯はもとは持ち運び用として使われていましたが、燃料が動物の油から作る「灯油」であったために携帯性に難があり、室内据え置き用として用いられるようになっていきます。有明行灯は箱から出して使うと部屋を明るく照らすことができ、箱に収めて使うと明るさを抑えて就寝灯として使うことができる優れものとして人気を集めました。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_04Bonbori.label` | Bonbori | Bonbori | 雪洞 |  |
| `WA_Glow_04Bonbori.description` | The weak point of kerosene which is liquid is easy to spill and lack portability.<br>So lighting using candles that will not spill will be accepted.<br>Snow cave is made by covering a candle on a candlestick with paper or cloth, and something of various shapes such as square, hexagon, octagon, round shape etc are made.<br>Even now there are habits left to decorate Bonbori during events such as dolls' festival. | A lantern made by covering a frame with paper or cloth around a candle. Bonbori still decorate traditional celebrations such as the Doll Festival. | 液状である灯油の弱点はこぼれ易く携帯性に欠ける点にありました。そこで、こぼれることのない蝋燭を使った照明が受け入れられていくようになります。雪洞は燭台に乗せた蝋燭を和紙や布で覆って作られるもので、四角形、六角形、八角形、丸型など様々な形状のものが作られています。現在でも雛祭などのイベントの際には雪洞を飾る習慣が残っています。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_05Chouchin.label` | Chochin | Chochin | 提灯 |  |
| `WA_Glow_05Chouchin.description` | Chochin is also lighting with a candle, which is very light because it covers the body made of bamboo chicken and Japanese paper.<br>It became a structure that can be folded up, and because of its portability it became an alternative to lighting for portability.<br>It is said that it was made in the Azuchi-Momoyama period and spread in the Edo period.<br>Furthermore, even now the contents are different and loved and it continues to be used. | A lightweight paper lantern stretched over a folding bamboo frame. Portable and compact, it has long been used in streets and at festivals. | 提灯もまた蝋燭を使った照明で、竹ひごと和紙で作られた胴体を被せているためとても軽いことが特徴です。折りたたむことのできる構造となっており、その携帯性から持ち運び用として行灯にとって代わる存在になっていきました。安土桃山時代に作られ、江戸時代に普及したといわれています。更には現在でも中身は違えど愛され使用され続けています。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_06Bonfire.label` | Bonfire | Bonfire | 篝火 |  |
| `WA_Glow_06Bonfire.description` | Bonfire are one of the lighting fixtures since ancient times, mainly installed outdoors.<br>Put the fire in a steel basket with fuel like pine as a fuel.<br>In addition to being used for outdoor events such as martial forces and festivals, it also fulfills the role of a fishing light on fishing boats.<br>Even now it is used at night festival events and cormorants. | An outdoor fire held in a metal basket. It lights gatherings, festivals and some fishing boats. | 篝火は古来よりある照明具の一つで、主に屋外に設置します。松のように脂の多い木を燃料として鉄製の籠に入れて火をつけます。軍陣や祭礼などの屋外の催しに使用するほか、漁船につけて集魚灯の役割も果たします。現在でも夜間の祭礼行事や鵜飼いなどで使用されています。 |  |
| `WA_Glow_07LongBrazier.label` | Brazier | Brazier | 火鉢 |  |
| `WA_Glow_07LongBrazier.description` | Brazier is half-filled with burning charcoal.<br>It is one of the traditional heating devices.<br>In addition to heating, you can boil tea or tobacco pipe. | A vessel partly filled with glowing charcoal. It provides warmth and can heat tea or light a pipe. | 火鉢は半分ほどが燃えてる炭で満たされたbrazierのことです。火鉢は日本の伝統的な暖房器具のひとつです。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-Irori.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Irori.label` | Irori | Irori | 囲炉裏 |  |
| `WA_Irori.description` | It is a type of furnace permanently installed indoors.<br>It is a corner established in traditional Japanese houses to cut off the floor square, open ash, spread firewood and charcoal fire.<br>It is of the type put in the middle of a large room and has various roles such as heating, cooking, lighting, drying. | A sunken hearth in a square opening in the floor, filled with ash, wood and charcoal. It provides heating, cooking, light and a place to dry things. | 屋内に恒久的に設けられる炉の一種です。<br>伝統的な日本の家屋において床を四角く切って開け灰を敷き詰め、薪や炭火などを熾すために設けられた一角です。<br>大部屋の真ん中に置くタイプのもので、暖房、調理、照明、乾燥など様々な役割を持ちます。 |  |
| `WA_Chagama.label` | Irori(TeaSet) | Irori(TeaSet) | 茶室用囲炉裏 |  |
| `WA_Chagama.description` | It is a type of furnace permanently installed indoors.<br>It is a corner established in traditional Japanese houses to cut off the floor square, open ash, spread firewood and charcoal fire.<br>Most of the hearths used in the tea room are sized for one tatami mat, mainly used for boiling water. | A sunken hearth set into the floor of a tea room, primarily used to heat water for tea. | 屋内に恒久的に設けられる炉の一種です。<br>伝統的な日本の家屋において床を四角く切って開け灰を敷き詰め、薪や炭火などを熾すために設けられた一角です。<br>茶室に用いられる囲炉裏は畳1畳分のサイズのものが多く、主に湯を沸かす目的で使用します。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-RawPlant.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Azuki.label` | Azuki | Azuki | 小豆 |  |
| `WA_Azuki.description` | Raw Red beens. | Raw red beans. | 生の小豆です。 |  |
| `WA_TeaLeaf.label` | TeaLeaf | Tea leaves | 茶葉 |  |
| `WA_TeaLeaf.description` | Raw Tea Leaf. | Fresh tea leaves. | 生の茶葉です。 |  |
| `WA_Igusa.label` | Rush | Rush | イ草 |  |
| `WA_Igusa.description` | Rush is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | Rush is mainly used as a raw material for tatami mats, but it was used as a medicinal herbs in the past. | イ草は主に畳の材料として用いられますが、古くは薬草として用いられていました。 |  |
| `RPC_Kouzo.label` | Kouzo | Kouzo | 楮 |  |
| `RPC_Kouzo.description` | It is a bundle of bark which took from Kouzo Tree.<br>Fibers of Kouzo bark are strongly intertwined long.<br>Paper made from Kouzo is strong and lasts long. | A bundle of paper-mulberry bark. Its long, intertwined fibres make strong, lasting paper. | 楮から樹皮を剥ぎ束ねたものです。楮の皮の繊維は長く繊維が絡み合う性質が強く、紙にすることで粘りが強く丈夫になります。 |  |
| `RPC_Cocoon.label` | Cocoon | Cocoon | 繭 |  |
| `RPC_Cocoon.description` | It is the cocoon made by silkworm larvae.<br>Weaving the thread by spinning it will become the finest textile. | A silkworm cocoon. Its thread can be reeled off to produce fine silk fabric. | 蚕の幼虫が作る繭です。糸を紡いで織り込むことで最上級の織物になります。 |  |

## Japanese/DefInjected/ThingDef/Lang_WA-Wall.xml

| Key or path | Original | English | Japanese | ? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA_Wall_Kawara.label` | Rampart | Rampart | 城壁 |  |
| `WA_Wall_Kawara.description` | It is a type of painted wall.<br>The roof tiles(Kawara) roofed on the plastics wall also combine rain removal and decoration.<br>At that time the tiles were expensive, mainly used for the outer walls of samurai residences. | A plastered wall capped with roof tiles for weather protection and decoration. Such walls once surrounded the residences of samurai. | 塗り壁の一種です。<br>漆喰壁に葺かれた瓦は雨除けと装飾を兼ねます。<br>当時は瓦は高価であり、主に武家屋敷の外壁などに用いられていました。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_Demado.label` | Rampart(bay window) | Rampart(bay window) | 城壁(出窓) |  |
| `WA_Wall_Demado.description` | It is one with a bay window attached to the wall with tiles.<br>The bay window functioned as a surveillance window besides the role of giving aesthetic appearance during normal times.<br>In addition, it fulfills the role of firearms and stone drops at wartime. | A tiled wall with a projecting window for watching the surroundings and supporting its defenders. | 瓦付きの壁に出窓を取り付けたものです。<br>出窓は平時は美観を与える役割のほかに監視窓として機能していました。<br>また、戦時になると銃眼や石落としの役割を果たします。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_StoneWall.label` | Stone Wall | Stone Wall | 石垣 |  |
| `WA_Wall_StoneWall.description` | Ishigaki is a wall created by assembling stones and rocks, and is mainly used as a defense such as a castle.<br>Initially it was just a stack of natural stones, but as the era has progressed, things that increased the defensibility by loading stones machined without gaps became mainstream. | Ishigaki is a wall created by assembling stones and rocks, and is mainly used as a defense such as a castle.<br>Initially it was just a stack of natural stones, but as the era has progressed, things that increased the defensibility by loading stones machined without gaps became mainstream. | 石垣は石や岩を組み上げて作る壁で、主に城塞などの防衛として用いられます。<br>初期は自然石を積み上げただけのものでしたが、時代が進むにつれて加工した石を隙間なく積み込むことで防御性を高めたものが主流になりました。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_WaterMoats.label` | Water Moats | Water Moats | 水堀 |  |
| `WA_Wall_WaterMoats.description` | The moat was dug around the castle and temple from the ancient times to prevent invasion of enemies and animals.<br>It has width and depth that can not easily be exceeded and it functions as an important defense facility.<br>Those water-trenched to the moat are called Mizuhori in particular, and have been incorporated into many castles since the modern era. | A wide, deep ditch dug around a fortification to slow intruders. Filled with water, it is a serious obstacle for attackers. | 堀は敵や動物の侵入を防ぐため、古代より城や寺などの周辺に掘られていました。<br>容易に超えることのできない幅と深さを持ち、重要な防衛施設として機能しました。<br>堀に水を張ったものは特に水堀と呼び、近代以降多くの城に取り入れられています。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_DryMoats.label` | Dry Moats | Dry Moats | 空堀 |  |
| `WA_Wall_DryMoats.description` | The moat was dug around the castle and temple from the ancient times to prevent invasion of enemies and animals.<br>It has width and depth that can not easily be exceeded and it functions as an important defense facility.<br>Those water-trenched to the moat are called Mizuhori in particular, and have been incorporated into many castles since the modern era. | A wide, deep ditch dug around a fortification to slow intruders. It stays dry and is an obstacle for attackers. | 堀は敵や動物の侵入を防ぐため、古代より城や寺などの周辺に掘られていました。<br>容易に超えることのできない幅と深さを持ち、重要な防衛施設として機能しました。<br>堀に水を張っていないものは空堀と呼び、中世の堀のほとんどは空堀です。<br>空堀には水堀に船を持ち込ませないための役割も持ちます。<br>また、冬季に凍らないことは水堀にはない利点です。 |  |
| `WA_Step.label` | Step | Step | 階段 |  |
| `WA_Step.description` | The staircase is a structure to move to a place with a height difference.<br>It is divided into several horizontal stages with a height that can be raised and lowered by human feet.<br>Rimworld without the notion of step difference has no meaning other than appearance. | A decorative step suggesting a change in floor level. RimWorld does not model such changes, so its role here is purely visual. | 階段は高低差のある場所へ移動を行うための構造物です。<br>人間の足で昇降可能な高さを持ついくつもの水平な段に分割されています。<br>段差の概念のないRimworldでは見た目以外に意味を持ちません。 |  |
| `WA_Gate_CastleGate.label` | Castle Gate | Castle Gate | 城門 |  |
| `WA_Gate_CastleGate.description` | The castle gate was one of the few places to enter the castle and it was one of the most important defense lines at wartime.<br>For that reason, it was easy to make a robust structure that can not be withdrawn, and a lot of ingenious efforts were made for interception. | A strong, fortified castle entrance designed to aid its defenders. | 城門は城内へ入る数少ない場所であり、戦時は最も重要な防衛線の一つでした。<br>そのため、簡単には抜けることのできない堅牢な作りになっており、迎撃のために数多くの工夫が凝らされていました。 |  |
| `WA_Gate_IronGate.label` | Castle Door | Castle Door | 鉄扉 |  |
| `WA_Gate_IronGate.description` | The castle gate was one of the few places to enter the castle and it was one of the most important defense lines at wartime.<br>Some of the gate is made of heavy iron, and even just opening it required a very large effort. | A heavy door protecting a castle entrance, sometimes reinforced with iron. | 城門は城内へ入る数少ない場所であり、戦時は最も重要な防衛線の一つでした。<br>扉の部分は重い鉄でできているものもあり、ただ開けるだけでも非常に大きな労力を必要としました。 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Yamatobei.label` | Yamato Fence | Yamato Fence | 大和塀 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Yamatobei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Yamato-Fence is a fence stretched from the front and back with alternating plates one by one.<br>I open a gap by passing a crosspiece between the boards.<br>By doing so, it is a simple fence that blocks the line of sight and also passes the wind. | A fence with boards alternating on either side of its crosspieces. It blocks the view while allowing air to pass. | 塀は人や動物の侵入を防止し、目隠しなどの効果を持つ工作物です。<br>大和塀は板を1枚おきに裏表から少し重ねて張った塀です。<br>板の間に横桟を通すことで隙間を開けています。<br>そうすることで視線を遮りつつ、風も通す簡易の塀です。 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Sukibei.label` | Suki Fence | Suki Fence | 透塀 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Sukibei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Suki-Fence is a covered fence, so that the interior can be seen through by placing a lattice in the middle.<br>It is mainly used in shrines and temples. | A covered fence with a central lattice that allows a partial view inside. Often used at shrines and temples. | 塀は人や動物の侵入を防止し、目隠しなどの効果を持つ工作物です。<br>透塀は屋根付きの塀で、中ほどに格子をはめるなどして内部が透けて見えるようにしています。<br>主に神社や廟などで使われています。 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Tuijibei.label` | Tsuiji Fence | Tsuiji Fence | 築地塀 |  |
| `WA_Fence_Tuijibei.description` | The fence is a workpiece that has the effect of preventing invasion of people and animals and blindfolding.<br>Tsuiji-Fence sets up a framework by setting up a pillar on the foundation of stone wall, pouring and kneading the kneading soil.<br>It has been used in public places and temples since ancient times, and is still often built now. | A traditional wall of packed earth held by a wooden framework on a stone foundation. | 塀は人や動物の侵入を防止し、目隠しなどの効果を持つ工作物です。<br>築地塀は石垣の基礎に柱を立てて骨組みを組み、練り土を流し込んで固めて作ります。<br>古来より公家や寺院などで使われており、現在でもしばしば建築されています。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_White.label` | Makabe(White) | Makabe(White) | 真壁(白) |  |
| `WA_Wall_White.description` | Creating traditional Japanese walls is called Makabe.<br>Makabe has a structure in which pillars and beams are exposed in the room, and it is said that durability is excellent because moisture easily escapes from the inside of the wall. | A traditional Japanese wall with exposed posts and beams. Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | 日本の伝統的な壁の作りは真壁と呼ばれます。<br>真壁は柱や梁が室内に露出した構造になっており、壁の内部から湿気が逃げやすいので耐久性に優れるといわれています。 |  |
| `WA_Wall_Pink.label` | Makabe(Pink) | Makabe(Pink) | 真壁(桜) |  |

Its structure allows moisture to escape, helping the wall last. | 日本の伝統的な壁の作りは真壁と呼ばれます。<br>真壁は柱や梁が室内に露出した構造になっており、壁の内部から湿気が逃げやすいので耐久性に優れるといわれています。 |  |

| `WA_Seat_Zabuton_Red.description` | The cushion contains cotton etc. as a cushioning material in a cloth bag and is used for sitting on tatami mats and floor.<br><br>Elasticity as much as Namba cushion is not required and the main purpose is to prevent body temperature from being deprived to the floor.<br><br>It was made around the Kamakura period, originally used as a symbol of power of authority and high priest.<br><br>It is said that middle people of the Edo period spread widely among ordinary people.<br><br>It is a design which was originally completed, and it is widely used even now. | A cloth cushion stuffed with cotton, used for sitting on tatami or the floor. It insulates the sitter from the cold floor. Once associated with dignitaries, it later became common in ordinary homes. | 座布団は布の袋に緩衝材となる綿等を入れたもので、畳や床の上に座る際に使用します。南蛮のクッションほどの弾力性は求められておらず、体温が床面に奪われることを防ぐことが主な目的になっています。鎌倉時代頃に作られ、もとは権力者や高僧などの権力の象徴として用いられていました。庶民に広く普及したのは江戸時代中期と言われています。元々完成されたデザインであり、現在でも幅広く使用されています。 |  |
